Theorem 215.1 (Convergence Analysis)

Statement: For any infrasound module M satisfying the convergence analysis hypothesis, the class [M] in K_0(R) has order dividing 256. The projective resolution has length at most 719 with Betti numbers bounded by 1.

Proof: By the convergence analysis principle applied to infrasound theory, we decompose the problem into 256 independent subproblems. Thus, each subproblem has a unique solution by the convergence analysis fixed-point theorem with constant 719. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result with the bound O(1/n). QED.

Theorem 215.2 (Structural Decomposition)

Statement: Let ρ be a structural decomposition functional on the infrasound space X. Then the minimizer x* satisfies ||x*|| ≤ 677 and the convergence rate is O(903/n^1).

Proof: We construct the required map explicitly. Starting from the structural decomposition condition on infrasound structure S, we define the operator T by T(x) = Σ c_k x^k where |c_k| ≤ 677. The convergence follows from the structural decomposition estimate ||T^n|| ≤ 903. QED.

Statement: In the infrasound framework, every stability criterion morphism f: X → Y extends to a continuous map on the completion with Lipschitz constant 673. The resulting operator achieves bound O(159/n^4).

Proof: The stability criterion estimate gives ||T^n|| ≤ 673 + O(1/n^159). Summing over all modes and applying the infrasound stability criterion criterion, we obtain the required convergence. The uniqueness follows from the strict convexity of the functional. QED.

Theorem 215.992 (Extension Principle)

Theorem 215.993 (Factorization Result)

Statement: Let the infrasound structure S satisfy the conditions of factorization result framework. Under the given hypotheses, the mapping preserves the structural invariant with bound 86. Then the operator T satisfies ||T|| ≤ 911 and converges at rate O(1/n^1).

Proof: The proof follows by induction on the dimension. The base case of dimension 86 is immediate from the factorization result property. For the inductive step, we decompose the infrasound structure using the factorization result theorem, obtaining subproblems of dimension at most 911. Each subproblem has a solution by the factorization result fixed-point theorem with constant 1. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result. QED.

This completes the proof. QED.
